          Title: Les Pseaumes mis en rime francoise, par Clement Marot, & Theodore de
                  Beze (the complete Genevan Psalter)
     Creator(s): Clement Marot (Versifier, Psalms 1-49 approx.)
                 Theodore de Beze (Versifier, remaining Psalms, completing the
                  collection)
                 Loys Bourgeois and others (original tune composers, not separately
                  credited on this title page)
    Print Basis: Geneva: Francois Perrin, pour Antoine Vincent, 1562 ("M.D.LXII", avec
                  privilege du Roy)
         Rights: Public Domain
       Language: fra
   Rights Basis: Published 1562; long out of US copyright, and the host's own metadata
                  marks it "Domaine public". Confirmed directly from the Internet
                  Archive scan (identifier BSG_8A817_2INV992RES_P1, Bibliotheque Sainte-
                  Genevieve copy), fetched and read in full this session.
Source Collection: Internet Archive (archive.org), identifier BSG_8A817_2INV992RES_P1
   Content note: Acquired for the Reformed Cities (Zurich/Geneva) world build, closing
                  Source Acquisition Manifest item G2 - the exact complete 1562 edition
                  named in Doc_01's own Practice-strand licensing request, not yet
                  actively searched before this pass. Confirmed genuine: royal printing
                  privilege text, and sequential "PSEAVME I/II/III..." headers running
                  through the collection. OCR quality is uneven in places (musical
                  notation interleaved with verse text on the original page layout
                  confuses the OCR layer more than a prose page would); the front matter
                  includes verse material addressed to persecuted believers, not only
                  the 150 Psalms themselves. Original French verse; no English
                  translation - this is a metrical psalter meant to be sung, not a prose
                  Bible translation, so no English equivalent is expected to exist.
     ______________________________________________________________________

NOTE ON THIS TRANSCRIPTION: the source is a raw OCR (djvu) text layer from
a scanned physical volume, not a clean digital edition. Runs of 2+ spaces
between words (a systematic OCR-layout artifact of the scan, not a feature
of the printed text) have been collapsed to single spaces throughout; no
word, spelling, or punctuation has been altered. OCR misreads may remain
uncorrected elsewhere in the file; any passage this build quotes verbatim
has been checked by eye against this file directly, not assumed correct
from the OCR alone.
